=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given a m x n 2D array board representing a chessboard, where board[i][j] represents the value of the cell (i, j).
Rooks in the same row or column attack each other. You need to place three rooks on the chessboard such that the rooks do not attack each other.
Return the maximum sum of the cell values on which the rooks are placed.
 
Example 1:

Input: board = [[-3,1,1,1],[-3,1,-3,1],[-3,2,1,1]]
Output: 4
Explanation:

We can place the rooks in the cells (0, 2), (1, 3), and (2, 1) for a sum of 1 + 1 + 2 = 4.

Example 2:

Input: board = [[1,2,3],[4,5,6],[7,8,9]]
Output: 15
Explanation:
We can place the rooks in the cells (0, 0), (1, 1), and (2, 2) for a sum of 1 + 5 + 9 = 15.

Example 3:

Input: board = [[1,1,1],[1,1,1],[1,1,1]]
Output: 3
Explanation:
We can place the rooks in the cells (0, 2), (1, 1), and (2, 0) for a sum of 1 + 1 + 1 = 3.

 
Constraints:

3 <= m == board.length <= 100
3 <= n == board[i].length <= 100
-10^9 <= board[i][j] <= 10^9

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python
class Solution:
    def maximumValueSum(self, board: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        
```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Implementation details:

```
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def maximumValueSum(self, board: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m = len(board)
        n = len(board[0])
        top = []
        for i in range(m):
            row = board[i]
            # get top 3 columns by value
            # Since n <=100, can sort:
            cols = sorted(range(n), key=lambda c: row[c], reverse=True)[:3]
            top.append([(row[c], c) for c in cols])
        ans = -10**30
        for i in range(m):
            for j in range(i+1, m):
                for k in range(j+1, m):
                    # compute best
                    best = -10**30
                    for val_i, ci in top[i]:
                        for val_j, cj in top[j]:
                            if cj == ci: continue
                            for val_k, ck in top[k]:
                                if ck == ci or ck == cj: continue
                                s = val_i + val_j + val_k
                                if s > best:
                                    best = s
                    if best > ans:
                        ans = best
        return ans
```

Now, let's think about if there is a more efficient way to compute best for each row triple using precomputed top3 values and columns. Our code computes best for every row triple from scratch. Could maybe precompute pair best for rows i,j and columns? Not needed.

Let's check code for syntax:
```
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def maximumValueSum(self, board: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m = len(board)
        n = len(board[0])
        top = []
        for i in range(m):
            row = board[i]
            cols = sorted(range(n), key=row.__getitem__, reverse=True)[:3]
            top.append([(row[c], c) for c in cols])
        
        ans = -10**30
        for i in range(m - 2):
            ti = top[i]
            for j in range(i + 1, m - 1):
                tj = top[j]
                for k in range(j + 1, m):
                    tk = top[k]
                    best = -10**30
                    for vi, ci in ti:
                        for vj, cj in tj:
                            if cj == ci:
                                continue
                            for vk, ck in tk:
                                if ck == ci or ck == cj:
                                    continue
                                s = vi + vj + vk
                                if s > best:
                                    best = s
                    if best > ans:
                        ans = best
        return ans
```
This uses `range(m - 2)` for i, `range(i + 1, m - 1)` for j, `range(j + 1, m)` for k. If m=3: i range(1) -> i=0; j range(1,2) -> j=1; k range(2,3) -> k=2. Good. If m=4: i=0, j=1,2, k accordingly; i=1? range(m-2)=range(2) -> i=0,1. For i=1, j range(2,3) -> j=2, k=3. Good. This enumerates all triples.

Could `best` remain -10**30? No, matching exists. But if due to bug, ans remains -10**30; not issue. But maybe if top lists size 3, matching exists. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "top3 per row" if the optimal solution uses a row's top3 column but another row's chosen column is not top3; we argued can transform all. Let's maybe formalize with an iterative algorithm:
Given optimal placement P with rows r1,r2,r3 and columns c1,c2,c3. For t=1..3:
- Consider row r_t. Let T_t be its top3 columns. Among T_t, at most two columns are used by the other two rows in the current placement, so there exists a column c'_t in T_t not used by any other row.
- Replace c_t with c'_t. Since c'_t not used by others, placement remains valid. Since c_t not in T_t? Wait, if c_t is already in T_t, we can leave it; but if c_t not in T_t, then board[r_t][c'_t] >= board[r_t][c_t] (actually if c_t not in T_t, all top3 values >= value at c_t). If c_t in T_t, no change needed. If c_t not in T_t but c'_t maybe equal, sum not decrease.
But what if c_t is in T_t but another row later takes it? We process in order and when replacing other rows, they choose columns not used by current rows, so they won't take c_t if it's currently used by row r_t. Thus c_t remains. So if c_t in T_t, it stays. If c_t not in T_t, we replace with a top3 column not used by others. But what if the only unused top3 column is already used by a previously processed row? Wait, "not used by any other row" includes previously processed rows. We choose not used by any other row, so it won't conflict. Good. At the end, each row's column is in its top3. Sum nondecreasing. Good.
But there is a subtlety: When processing row r_t, if c_t is not in T_t, we choose c'_t in T_t not used by other rows. But what if all c'_t in T_t that are not used by other rows have value less than c_t? Impossible because c_t not in T_t means there are at least 3 columns with value >= c_t; T_t contains three of them. Since c_t not in T_t, all T_t values >= c_t. So any c'_t has value >= c_t. Good.
If c_t is in T_t, we can leave it, but what if c_t is also used by another row? In a valid placement, no. Later rows won't take it. Good.
Thus proof solid.

</think>

=== ANSWER ===
```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def maximumValueSum(self, board: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m = len(board)
        n = len(board[0])

        # For each row, only its top 3 values can ever be needed.
        top = []
        for i in range(m):
            row = board[i]
            cols = sorted(range(n), key=row.__getitem__, reverse=True)[:3]
            top.append([(row[c], c) for c in cols])

        ans = -10**30

        # Enumerate all triples of rows.
        for i in range(m - 2):
            ti = top[i]
            for j in range(i + 1, m - 1):
                tj = top[j]
                for k in range(j + 1, m):
                    tk = top[k]
                    best = -10**30

                    for vi, ci in ti:
                        for vj, cj in tj:
                            if cj == ci:
                                continue
                            for vk, ck in tk:
                                if ck == ci or ck == cj:
                                    continue
                                s = vi + vj + vk
                                if s > best:
                                    best = s

                    if best > ans:
                        ans = best

        return ans
```
